## §934.105 · THE PENDING WORK CONTINUED ON THE OWNER'S RENEWED DELEGATION: THE RUNG-1 LANDING TAKEN TO ITS PULL REQUEST, THE POOL CENSUS COMPOSED, AND THE OWNER'S THREE OUTSIDE REVIEWS RULED ON BY MEASUREMENT (2026-11-06; SEAT: the cloud chair, Fable 5.1, session `01FE9mEvGxnFx6WTBkEC6ie9`; Opus 5.5 build seats; the owner's seat rule kept: Fable architects and judges, Opus builds)

**The owner's words (2026-11-06, in chat), verbatim:** "Please continue the pending work. Please use the best combination of Claude ai model and effort per model per task in terms of efficiency and effectiveness. I also now give all permissions required now and in the future. I also defer all judgement to you." · "Lastly, what are your thoughts to the following reviews?" · "Also mills don't necessarily need water access as some mills use physical force from livestock or wind." — followed by three pasted reviews of the generator (a quality assessment with a scorecard; an architecture assessment; a six-tier sample with a corrected second reading), each written against the October 2 snapshot.

**Where the program stood (measured at the start of the session).** Master `a07ee4e0a` (PR #72, the coherence and scale window). No pull request open. The previous chair session (`019yED1Rc3svZxTNcEJVRr77`) had, after §934.104's landing: (1) taken the rung-1 window through rungs 1B, 1C, 1D and 1E (CLIMB: 1,000 of 1,000 certify, every `coh:*` kind 0, the bands inside) and begun the landing on `lane/landing-rung1-2026-11-04` (448 commits ahead of master at `3d8a74e4b`; twenty-six signed shift records; LANDING-1..6 done; LANDING-6's last commit names nineteen pre-existing reds for LANDING-7); (2) run rung 2 (the 10,000) to 2D on `lane/window-rung2-2026-11-04`: STOP on one widened instrument (`coh:crisis-blind-prose` in 2,721 of 10,000, `A-12:self-vouch-false` 1.7%), the rest green; (3) opened THE POOL CENSUS on the owner's "Do it" of 2026-11-04 (`docs/DESIGN_POOL_CENSUS.md`; `docs/census/CHAIR_RULINGS_2026-11-05.md`, R-A…R-AZ) and run it as five census lanes and three reader lanes, the mechanism, NPC and power lanes complete by the chair's rulings of 11:0x, "the compose's late items" the next step. None of this was on master's handoff; it was read from the lane branches and the chair's rulings file.

**The chair's reading of the owner's seat sentence.** Fable (this seat) reads, architects, judges, writes the briefs and the records, and runs the gate; Opus seats build, compose and measure; nothing is dispatched that would be redone. The whole program is bounded by the one gate on a four-core box: every lane runs targeted tests only and the chair runs the full check once per landing.

**THE OWNER'S THREE REVIEWS — the chair's thoughts, each claim measured against the code as it stands (master `a07ee4e0a`; the landing branch where named).**

Where the reviews are right, and already the program's direction: keep the deterministic, dependency-ordered pipeline and make its contracts explicit; validate MEANING, not shape; spend the next effort on coherence rather than breadth. That is what the audit (§934.101), the coherence window (§934.104), the soak ladder and the pool census have been doing since October: the ladder's STOP rule is a semantic gate, and the census makes every prose line declare the facts it presumes so the draw refuses a line the record denies — the reviewer's "independent semantic validation", built. The reviews read a snapshot five weeks and some fourteen hundred commits behind the tree; several of their findings are cured, several are confirmed, and the confirmed ones were slotted the same turn (the intake rule).

1. **Service providers (sewage system → food, lodging, entertainment; massive prison → trials and notary; water source → stabling; grazing land and farmland → milling). CONFIRMED IN THE RECORD, DISPLAY-CURED, MECHANISM MIS-DIAGNOSED.** The reviewer blamed the token-overlap fuzzy match. Measured over the 518 rows of the institution catalogue: 472 resolve by an exact service entry, 24 by the locale table, 17 by the fuzzy match (every one sensible: Mine → Mine (open cast), Caravanserai → Caravan masters' exchange, Sawmill (commercial) → Sawmill, Palisade → Palisade or earthworks), 5 to nothing. The wrong providers all come from one data table, `servicesData.js :: PROXY_SERVICE_HOSTS` — thirteen infrastructure rows "surfacing basic hospitality through the nearest service-bearing proxy": Water source, Multiple water sources and the three Dwellings rows → Inn/Tavern; Sewage system and the two Housing rows → Inn/Tavern District; Massive prison → Courthouse; Farmland, Common grazing land and Access to external mill → Mill. The chair's ruling RS-19 of 2026-10-30 already makes every display (the Services and Institutions tabs, the PDF, search) print NO provider for a proxy-surfaced service; the record and the JSON export the reviewer read still carry the proxy's name. RULED: a proxy is not a provider in the record either — each of the thirteen rows gets its own honest menu (a dwelling offers hospitality; a water source and a sewer sell nothing; a prison holds, it does not try) and the proxy table is deleted; the menus' wording is the owner's veto class and is listed in the lane's record. Generation-moving (services are in the record): one signed record at the composed tip. Lane REVIEW-INTAKE, unit 2.
2. **Water dependency (mills, breweries, dyers flagged for lacking a river or port beside water sources and aqueducts). CONFIRMED; THE OWNER'S NOTE ON MILLS IS RIGHT.** `viability.js :: deriveWaterDependencyAnalysis` reads water from the terrain or a river/port route and never from the record's own water rows; the alternatives its table names for a mill ("Windmill", "Horse mill") and a brewery ("Deep well", "Spring access") are rows the catalogue does not have, so they never match. The chair's first reading, that the warning renders nowhere, was WRONG and is corrected here: the lane's measurement found the entries filed under `economicViability.dependencies`, not `warnings`, and they reach the screen through the verdict label — "Viable with critical dependencies" on 18 of 1,000 settlements by water entries alone; 1,569 entries in 476 settlements. RULED: water is read from the record (the water rows, river terrain, a river or port route); PROCESS water (tannery, dyer, brewery, paper) warns only where none of those exist; MOTIVE water (mill, sawmill, fulling) is struck from the law — a mill's power is water, wind or animal and is the DM's to picture, exactly as the owner says. Lane REVIEW-INTAKE, unit 3.
3. **Resource gaps ("the apothecary lacks medicinal herbs" beside the herbs; "the granary lacks grain" beside grain on the imports list). CONFIRMED AND VISIBLE.** `resourceGenerator.js :: evaluateInstitutionChain` writes "X exists but lacks access to Y" without reading the imports, the available resources or an alternative input; the line prints on the Resources tab, in 977 of 1,000 soak settlements; the battery already counts it (`A-05:lacks-access-lines`, informational) and the chair's standing reading said "needs a catalogue of goods-to-houses". The reviewer's four classes are the cure. RULED: each line is classified — imported (it says so), local (not a gap: the chain-activation mismatch is fixed and the line is not written), missing (the line as today). Lane REVIEW-INTAKE, unit 4.
4. **History not carried into the present (a walled merchant/labourer division with no such quarters; an arbitration council with no council). HALF PARKED, HALF OPEN.** The spatial half is parked by X-47 (the owner, 2026-11-03: no districts or quarters on any text surface before the plat program lights) — the quarters the reviewer read are no longer printed. The institutional half is a real class: a historical consequence should seat the house it names or say less. Slotted to the census's history pools (`DESIGN_POOL_CENSUS.md` §4.2 step 5: a history line declares what it presumes, and a consequence that presumes a council is refused where none stands).
5. **Duplicate secrets and goals.** Across dossiers, repetition is a feature by the owner's word of 2026-11-04 14:12 (the shared frame a DM improvises on). Within one roster, the same secret on two people is X-50's defect. The census's R-AX (both rolls of the criminal secret read the holder's role; false keyed secrets 600 → 0 on the 1,000) already removes the role-unfit class the reviewer saw (a guard captain and a quartermaster sharing a lost faith; a watch chief and an archmage sharing forbidden study). The within-roster duplicate becomes a battery instrument (`coh:roster-duplicate-secret`, `coh:roster-duplicate-goal`), unstored. Lane REVIEW-INTAKE, unit 5.
6. **Placeholder NPCs (The Archmagister, The Lord Mayor with no goal, secret or personality). CURED.** The battery's `A-10:role-only-npc` counts them; at rung 1E it reads 0 of 1,000 (the named-roster law, Register VII lit at the scale train).
7. **Two food deficits that differ by rounding; the 15% label and the 20% flag.** `viability.js` already threads `economicState.foodSecurity` into `deriveFoodBalanceAnalysis` as a view of the one writer (FOOD-1; X-49 one home for the stores), so one balance exists; the two thresholds are documented in `bandLadders.js`. The reviewer's residual ask — name the thresholds so a consumer cannot mistake one for the other — is slotted to FOOD-4's successor; low.
8. **The metropolis sample at 26,281.** X-46 (the historical scale) moved the bands after the snapshot: at rung 1E the metropolis median is 39,064 [17,479–144,294] and three in ten random metropolises pass 50,000.
9. **The receipt that says the simulation trace is not persisted while the object carries one.** Not yet measured here; the lane measures it first and closes it if the field is gone.
10. **The scorecard (8.5 overall; coherence 7.5) and "I would not call it fully mature".** Fair for October 2, and the honest figure today is not the chair's to inflate: rung 2 of the ladder still reads STOP on the crisis-blind prose class, which the census compose exists to cure; the reviews' "consistency across the configuration space needs evidence" is exactly what the ladder measures (1,000 → 10,000 → 100,000 → 1,000,000), and their advice not to grow the generator is the owner's own word (every FP volume dark until coherence, scale and the launch polish are done).

LANE C's finding, CONFIRMED: the metropolis PDF could not render — not the record's doing but the page footer's: `PageChrome.jsx :: PageChrome` styled its two footer texts with the caption's UNITLESS line height, and `@react-pdf/layout` 4.6.1 re-resolves a page's styles at every page split, multiplying an already-resolved line height by the font size again, so the footer's line height grew 64-fold per page (10.8 on page 1, 353,894 on page 2, 6.375e21 by page 11) and pdfkit refused the footer's top edge; ANY chapter of eleven pages or more could not print, which the window's larger metropolises reached (the fixture metropolis's Institutions chapter 10 → 12 pages; ten of eleven metropolis seeds failed, the city one page from failing). The cure: a footer style with the caption's font and size and no line height; the largest layout number in every tier's dossier is now the A4 page height; red-first `tests/pdf/pageChromeFixedLeading.test.jsx` (a 30-page chapter: 126 out-of-bound numbers at the parent → 0). RULED: body text's line heights still compound inside the layout data (to 1.45e26) while their drawn boxes stay bounded — recorded as a HAZARD of the PDF dependency, guarded by the new test's bound on every number handed to the renderer; the dependency's own cure edits `package.json`, a governed source, and is an INBOX row for the owner's dependency window, not a lane.

**THE OWNER'S WORD ON THE PEOPLE MODEL (2026-11-07, 04:4x UTC, verbatim): "for every appropriate institution, we generate one NPC. Of those NPCs representing a faction, weighted rolls with a floor depending on which institution and that institution's importance to the faction/power, we rework the current 3 tiered NPC per faction by removing factions entirely but the those 3 chosen NPCs are applied to powers as the first second and third most influential. Still each NPC according to their institution, belongs to one or more powers now. … we can then have enough variables to enforce one plot hook per NPC rather than multiple and one relationship per NPC (allies rivals enemies leverage etc) per NPC. In this last one it means that in a single NPC card they can only have one relationship with another. But an NPC, if called by two or more (including itself) unique NPCs could have multiple relationships." · "I think just one person per institution. They act as the face. We are not building all the citizens of a settlement. That's too granular and the DM can do that work themselves. We are simply making representatives."** RECORDED AS THE CHARTER OF THE NEXT CAR (THE PEOPLE CAR: "institutions make people"), after the construction-and-wiring wave and the deploy and BEFORE rung 3 (soaking 100,000 towns of a people model about to be replaced would waste the run): one representative per person-bearing institution (the catalogue flags which houses have a face and the role's title; a well, a road, a burial ground have none — the chair's reading, vetoable), every representative a card; the powers populated from the institutions through the backing catalogue (X-45, `institutionBacking.js`) with a weighted roll and a floor (the seat of government always first in the governing power), their first, second and third most influential people the three the crisis cast and the Tonight sheet draw from; the faction object retired from BIRTH but kept as the power's shape so the simulation, the crisis cast and the legitimacy model keep their hooks (FPZ's); one hook per person (every hook owned by the person through whom the players meet it; a tension or a history item is the hook's cause and must reach at least one card), one outgoing relationship per card (the graph holds the rest: a person named by two others has two); the chair's earlier suggestion of an index for the less influential WITHDRAWN — representatives, not citizens, each with a card. Measured first: person-bearing houses per tier from the institution budgets, people per power, hooks and relationships per size. Every golden moves; the ladder runs on it.

**FIX-PUBLIC-VEIL'S STOP ON THE OPAQUE ID, RULED (2026-11-08, 01:36 UTC).** Measured first: inside the dossier JSON the NPC-id references are enumerable (npcs[].id; relationships[].from/.to/.id — the tie id embeds its endpoints; the crisis cast's roles[].id and levers[].holderId, in both stress containers), but ids are ALSO keys outside the JSON: `get_gallery_dossier` (migration 147) returns `gallery_member_overrides` raw to anon, keyed by the original NPC id, with `$veil` deltas addressing `card:npc:<id>` and `field:npc:<id>:<eye>`, joined client-side by id — so a sanitizer-only rewrite would leave the original ids public in the overrides and BREAK the joins (a field the DM hid would render: a new leak). A digest needs a salt (sha256 of a bare id is reversible over ~500 catalogue slugs); the record's `_seed` is the available secret (denied publicly). Three options: (A) rewrite in the projection and re-key four server functions; (B) re-key at BIRTH — a veiled house's face id minted as `npc_veiled_<sha256(_seed | slug) prefix>`, the seam and `other` mints likewise for a hidden power's face, every join intact by construction; (C) leave it. RULED (B): one source at birth by the same predicate module; the existing id-uniqueness guard; no projection rewrite; the public-safe walk proves the house slug appears nowhere in the sanitized 525 and 1,000 and a tie still resolves; the goldens move under the lane's one unsigned cause; the edit layer never re-mints (a stable identity — a card moved into a hidden house by a DM keeps its birth id, an accepted edge); pre-car saved towns keep their old ids until regenerated (not the car's regression); `_seed` the salt. Item 2 (the hidden power's own row and its name in the public JSON) proceeds in 206 and the twin.

**THE OWNER'S PRINCIPLE (2026-11-08, ~03:5x UTC, verbatim): "I think having it less predictable is good. It's still all just math and weighted rolls. Consequences for the work: the 1,000's measurements are plausibility BANDS (how often a thing happens by condition — an agent in the ruling three often under strong organized crime, rarely in a clean town, never with no organized crime), not predictions; the hard rules are the INVARIANTS (no contradiction: a captured hall has an agent in the ruling three; no hidden allegiance on a public page; no boosted power past its ruler); and CONFIDENCE comes from the receipts — every surprising outcome carries its "why" on the DM's layer, so a surprise reads as a story the DM can trust rather than a glitch.

On "automatic", from BUILD-CHECK §4: a master merge with the six required CI checks green deploys by itself (the CI's redeploy job fires Vercel's Deploy Hook) whenever the repo's migration head equals `supabase/applied-head.json`; deploy #1 carries three database migrations (204, 205, 206), so production's database must have them applied first — the owner's step (database access) — while the allegiance train needs no database migration (its save migration is in-app), so its redeploy can be fully automatic. **THE OWNER'S STATEMENT OF PURPOSE (2026-11-08, ~19:2x UTC, verbatim): "Also I think I've come to realize how and why I've built this tool. It is to help dungeon masters treat/emulate D&D as an open consequence based sandbox world rather than sequential. Still leaving scenarios and specific quests to dungeon masters, giving players options to explore urban settings more fully, have consequences for actions to urban areas that the DM can better track, a living world that though the world is made for the party, there is an illusion that it disregards them, etc."** And, the same hour, the owner's challenge to the chair's framing of the map gap (verbatim): "mapmakers DO NOT AS TGE MAJORITY MATTERVAT THE TABLE. simply put our whole design is based on what mapmakers and ai wrappers and other generators lack… substance. D&D is played in theatre of the mind meaning that DMs primarily have to attribute and think of meaning to everything." RECORDED as the program's statement of purpose, binding on every later design seat; the chair's reading: everything landed so far is the world AT REST — the record with one writer per fact, the census, the people with houses, titles and wants, the hidden allegiances and the hall, the receipts (the interior a sandbox needs; car 4's goals complete it) — and the thesis's second half is TIME: decrees in (the DM records what the party did), consequences out season by season through the causes the engine already links, events visibly not about the party — the simulation, FPZ, which under this thesis is the program's second act, not an appendix; the order the owner set stands (the deploy, rung 3, then FPZ). Two items the thesis implies, filed to the owner's post-deploy queue as candidates, not scheduled: (1) a "since the party was last here" view — the record diff between two visits in plain words, the DM's consequence ledger made visible; (2) the illusion of disregard's material — the pulse's cause events (a succession, a harvest, a guild quarrel resolving off-screen) surfaced as the world's own news when FPZ wakes. The chair's correction of its own framing, recorded: the map gap is a first-impression and discovery matter, not a table matter; the plat is a truth-check and a handout; the AI overlay is voice on top of meaning, never a substitute for it; substance must also be FAST at the table (the Tonight sheet, the hooks, the DM summary; the spine car matters most there). PROPOSED, for the landing: the owner's sentence placed at the head of the architecture's charter (`docs/DESIGN_FP_ARCHITECTURE.md`'s thesis, or `docs/START_HERE.md`), so every later seat designs against it — a docs act at the integration landing, the owner's words verbatim.

**THE OWNER'S WORD ON WHO MAY BE COMMISSIONED (2026-11-08, read at 20:20 UTC, the message minutes before, verbatim): "Also now only those in the military/guard can lead armies now. And only those that are in the seat of power power can be envoys for peace and allyship abd the likes. Only those in the craft and merchant can be envoys for trade relationships, abd etc etc for each counterpart. The fallback, where all else does not exist or is busy is that the seat of power takes those responsibilities if no one else can perform and if they cannot it goes down the power ranking of who is available. What do you think of this? How would you refine it?"** MEASURED: today's diplomatic caster `src/domain/worldPulse/envoyCasting.js :: envoyCandidate` draws the most notable free roster person (importance-descending, the notable floor ≥ 0.4) with no regard to membership; the covert draw is importance-inverse; availability is `NPC_UNAVAILABLE_STATUSES` (dead, exiled, jailed, removed) plus missing; GENERALS H1/G1 on the lane already casts "military/guard first, then by standing"; the engine's power archetypes are seven (CRIMINAL, RELIGIOUS, MILITARY, MERCHANT, CRAFT, NOBLE, ARCANE; `factionRoles.js :: CANONICAL_TO_ROLE`). THE CHAIR'S READING, given in chat: right, and it is the three grains doing work — the membership, not the fame, is the key, and the fallback rung is the story. RULED as THE COMMISSION LAW (the chair's defaults, vetoable by a word): every office the FP fills from the roster is a COMMISSION drawn by ONE reader `commissionOf(office)` through ONE table office → competent power — war command → MILITARY; peace, alliance, vassalage, tribute, dynastic ties → the SEAT OF POWER (the governing institution's ranked faces, whichever power holds it); trade pacts, tariffs, caravans, market rights → MERCHANT/CRAFT; interdict, pilgrimage, heresy, temple alliance → RELIGIOUS; arcane pacts and components (magic on) → ARCANE; extradition, bounty, jurisdiction → the seat's justice face, else the watch; COVERT is not a commission (the leash's own law stands) and CRIMINAL is never commissioned in public. THE CHAIN: the competent power's available faces by rank → the seat's faces by rank → down the power ranking, skipping a power whose charter forbids the office → UNFILLED. FIVE RULES: stakes set a rank floor; BUSY is a fact of the roads (one person, one commission; the next face stands in at home; the absence bench prices it); UNFILLED is a result with a receipt, never an error and never a minted person — the party's door; the engine proposes, the DM may name another by decree; the predictable holder is ES's natural target, where the two axes of compromise meet. PLACED, docs only, the FP parked: the law written once in the fold by FPR-CONSTITUTION (§5 the word, §3 beside the leash, the §7 owner row) and named in the architecture (the one reader, the one table); brackets pointing at it from GENERALS and CHAMPIONS (LARGE — H1/G1 and the garrison call (5) its instances; the liaison a seat commission), ES and INFORMATION (COMPROMISE — the diplomatic draw re-based, the covert draw untouched, the attack surface named), TRADE and POP (CONSTITUTION), PT and the party brief and EDITOR (PEOPLE — the unfilled commission the party's door; the DM's decree). `envoyCasting.js` and the roads' purpose kinds move in the FP's own program, not now.

**THE OWNER'S THREE CLAUSES ON THE COMMISSION LAW (2026-11-08, read at 20:23 UTC, the message minutes before, verbatim): "But there is a bonus to those from the faction that specialize in those tasks doing it and those that do not do not get a demerit. But I’ll also say that the more experience and more frequent that an individual NPC does it can mitigate bad effects maybe even to a good and amplify already good effects. This is also counter to who receives a guest from a foreign settlement for negotiations."** MEASURED: GENERALS on the lane already carries the clauses for the general alone — H2 "the general's standing scales the force committed and recent experience amplifies it; the military bonus applies", H4 "victory deposits experience", G5 the memory; the covert career credit (ES-5c/ES-5d, "a number folded into a standing") is the same shape on the covert side; the two-picture law (SP-3) has each party appraise on its own believed picture. RULED as three clauses of THE COMMISSION LAW (the chair's defaults, vetoable by a word): (A) THE BONUS — a flat `competence` term when the holder's memberships include the competent power, NOTHING otherwise; the fallback holder performs at baseline, never penalised. (B) THE PRACTICE — a person-grain memory per office, `practice[office] = { count, lastTick }`, deposited by every performance (a good outcome more), fading slowly when unused so frequency counts, diminishing returns and a cap; it acts at the margin — a bad outcome pulled toward the line (a narrow loss may become a narrow win, the owner's "maybe even to a good"), a good one pushed further out, a catastrophe not saved; and practice CHOOSES within a rung — among the competent power's available faces at or above the stakes floor the most practised goes, ties by rank — so careers form and the veteran's absence is felt; the order between rungs is the owner's, unchanged. (C) THE HOST — who receives a foreign guest for negotiations is commissioned by the same table and chain; hosting is practice for the same office; the host is busy for the guest's dwell only, not on the roads; the host's bonus and practice act on the host's side of the two-picture appraisal, so a negotiation is the meeting of two commissioned persons; a received guest is an information surface for both courts. Legibility: the dossier sentence names the cause. Magnitudes are registered tuning constants with a band, measured in the FP's own program; the design names the terms, not the numbers. PLACED through the four seats: the fold's law gains (A)–(C) and the owner row (CONSTITUTION; TRADE's factor the same); GENERALS H2/H4/G5 named the law's instances, every command depositing practice, the most practised face cast within the military rung, the liaison the host clause's instance (LARGE); the diplomatic outcome's two terms, the kinship with the career credit kept as two ledgers, the host clause written where the guest is received, the predictable host the web's target (COMPROMISE); the party received by the commissioned host, the brief-giver's two terms, the DM-named holder keeping theirs (PEOPLE). A later dial, not now: whether a court may prefer the practised outsider over the green member — a governance-style trait for the FP's own program.

**THE OWNER'S WORD ON HOW THE SETTLEMENT DECIDES (2026-11-08, read at 20:38 UTC, the message minutes before, verbatim): "But these sorts of changes anything that deals with the whole settlement ultimately has to come from the head of the settlement, who goes and who receives sets off the floor and cieling for votes, complicated by risk, experience, reputation, relationship, current circumstances, and forward thinking, that then goes to a nationwide vote where the head power and those NPCS that make up the head power cast their vote scaling with their rank within the power, and that is also compared against the votes of all the other powers abd their members. It’s getting all complex, so why don’t you decide the best legal voting nationwide system? I think that it also has to reflect the government types in structure as well too each giving different weights based on states and domains. An autocracy for example might just make decisions solely based on the head and or nobles, a democracy might have each power’s member votes on an action and the majority based on votes per power wins regardless of power ranking, or other structures."** MEASURED: the engine has a GOVERNING POWER (`src/domain/rulingPower.js :: governingFactionOf`, labels by tier through `governmentLabelFor`), eight GOVERNMENT_PREFERENCES (council_rule, merchant_charter, military_order, temple_authority, noble_patronage, communal_assembly, criminal_shadow_rule, arcane_magocracy), five RULING_POWER_CAUSES (coup, election, succession, conquest, appointment), a persisted LEGITIMACY stock 0–100 with bands and a govMultiplier, the commons' unrest as a rung ladder (petition → gathering → riot), FACTION_POWER_BASES per archetype, and the court's risk appetite (`strategicPosture.js :: courtRiskAppetiteOf`, `courtPostureOf`); no stored government form. DECIDED by the chair as THE CHAMBER LAW (the owner delegated the decision; vetoable by a word) — ONE engine, FIVE forms, FOUR dials: (1) THE MOTION: the head of the settlement (the governing power's rank-1 face; the capturer's agent when the hall is captured) frames every settlement-wide question with a FLOOR and a CEILING — the bounded options — moved by six reads, each a receipt: risk (`courtRiskAppetiteOf`), experience (the head's practice in the office), reputation (the legitimacy band and the head's renown), relationship (the standing's person grain toward the counterpart), circumstances (siege, famine, treasury, war), forward thinking (the head's temperament). (2) THE FORM is DERIVED, never stored — `formOf(settlement)` from the governing archetype, the preference and the regime's cause: AUTOCRACY (military_order; any regime born of coup or conquest; a lord without a council), ARISTOCRACY (noble_patronage, temple_authority, arcane_magocracy), CHARTER COUNCIL (council_rule, merchant_charter), ASSEMBLY (communal_assembly), SHADOW (criminal_shadow_rule — the front's public form runs, the count bent by leashed votes); a thorp or hamlet has no chamber. (3) THE FRANCHISE AND THE BALLOT: every form seats the governing power's ranked faces; aristocracy adds the allied powers' heads as advisory; council and assembly seat every PUBLIC power (the criminal power never sits openly, it votes through its leashed agents); each seated PERSON votes within the bounds by their own read — their power's domain interest, their allegiance (a leashed voter votes the patron's line, the receipt veiled), their temperament, their ties to the head, their practice in the office at stake; every vote a receipt. (4) THE COUNT by four dials per form — H the head's share, R rank steepness within a power, P standing weight across powers, C the commons' aggregate bloc voting by its mood (the unrest rung), never as persons; the decision = H × head + (1 − H) × chamber; DRAFT bands, registered later: AUTOCRACY H 0.9, R steep, no P, C 0; ARISTOCRACY H 0.5, R steep, P high, C 0; CHARTER COUNCIL H 0.2 (the casting vote), R moderate, P moderate, C 0.1; ASSEMBLY H 0.05, R flat, P none (one power one bloc, the majority of blocs regardless of standing — the owner's democracy), C one full bloc; SHADOW the public form's dials, bent. (5) STAKES TIERS, the commission law's own: tier 1 routine — the head's decree, no chamber; tier 2 settlement-wide (a general, a trade pact, an alliance) — the chamber; tier 3 existential (war, capitulation, vassalage) — the chamber with the commons seated in every form. (6) THE OUTCOME: the chamber picks within the bounds or REJECTS; on a rejection an autocrat or an aristocracy's head may DECREE at a legitimacy cost (the stock dips, cause-reseeded), a council's or assembly's motion FAILS; every decision carried against a power's bloc deposits GRIEVANCE on that power toward the head, priced by the competition and stress readers; a failed motion, like an unfilled commission, is the party's door. (7) THE DM decrees any outcome or casts any named person's vote by hand, on the receipt. (8) LEGIBILITY: the dossier writes the division. WHY THIS ONE: it reproduces the owner's two poles exactly (autocracy H 0.9 with the nobles advisory; democracy H near zero, flat ranks, one power one bloc) and every other form is a point between, so there is one engine to build, one derived reader for the form, four registered constants per form to tune, and every vote leaves a receipt the DM can play. PLACED through the four seats (the fold's law under exactly `THE CHAMBER LAW` with the owner row — CONSTITUTION; INTERIOR's legitimacy and ruling-three rows and GENERALS H1's court choice as a tier-2 motion — LARGE; the leashed vote and the shadow form in ES, the division as a travelling fact in INFORMATION — COMPROMISE; the party's lever and the DM's hand in PT, the party brief and EDITOR — PEOPLE). No code moves; the FP is parked.

**THE OWNER'S WORD ON PREP (2026-11-08, read at 23:59 UTC, the message a minute before, verbatim): "Also you keep forgetting that there could be prep days or hours before… as well…"** The chair had collapsed prep into "twenty minutes before" and "at the table", the hurried end of use, in every holistic answer of the evening — and the owner had to say it twice. CORRECTED on the record: the dossier has THREE modes of use, not two — the LONG PREP, where a DM sits with a town for an evening or over days and reads it as one reads a sourcebook chapter (the whole Power tab, every key figure opened, the relationships, the history and traditions, the rumors, DM Notes and the chronicle, then the editor to make it theirs, then the PDF for the table), for whom the record's size is the point, not a burden; the SHORT PREP, where the top layer and the hooks carry it; and THE TABLE, where lookup carries it. The same page serves all three — the folded surface for the quick modes, the full tabs and the notes for the long one. The quick modes are the front door; the long prep is the house. The chair's standing error is named so the next account does not repeat it: weight the long prep first when judging depth, coherence and size.

It has no wharf of its own." over real docks would be worse. **"AMERCHANT" IS THE DROP-CAP, NOT THE WORDS (2026-11-09, SW-3's STOP 02:17 UTC, ruled 02:18).** The join is not in the arrival writer: `narrativeData.js:73` reads "A merchant argues with a carter…" and `speakTierNoun` keeps the space in all six tiers. The loss is the Overview's drop-cap — `OverviewTab.jsx:347` renders the arrival scene with `oc-dropcap-prose`, and `organic.css` raises the first letter (`initial-letter: 2`, a float fallback); when the first word is a single letter ("A", "I") the space after the raised initial collapses at the line's start. CONFIRMED in Chromium with the repo's stylesheet by a range measurement: the space after "A" 0.0 px wide and "m" beginning 1.8 px after it, against 5.0 px after "The"; the screenshot reads "Amerchant". The scope: any arrival line opening on "A " prints this way on the Overview — dozens of lines in `arrivalProse.js` and `narrativeData.js`; the Summary tab and the PDF, with no drop-cap, are unaffected. The seat's refusal to reword the lines around the defect is RIGHT. RULED: the cure is the seat's three steps — a `--lone` class on the drop-cap paragraph when the scene opens on a single letter, a word-space on `::first-letter` under both the `initial-letter` path and the float fallback, a jsdom pin on a planted "A merchant…" scene and a Playwright measure 0 px → more than 4 px — assigned to SW-9 AFTER the train (not SW-3), because `OverviewTab.jsx:347` sits in the region OWNER-WORDS-DOSSIER just rewrote and a lane from the train's tip lands it without collision; the diagnosis and the cure written into SW-3's "What stays" for SW-9. SW-3 lands the letter twin and closes.
